# What we know, what we believe, and what we got wrong

Same ledger discipline as the earlier research repo's `docs/known.md` (which itself credits socom-unzipped's
`docs/KNOWN.md` for the pattern). Rules:

- **Proven** rows name the artefact that proves them. No artefact → not proven.
- **Believed** rows name the experiment that would settle them. No experiment → the row doesn't belong here.
- **Retracted** rows stay forever, with what killed them.
- IDs are permanent; never renumber, never reuse. This repo's own IDs start at `N1`; rows pulled in
  from the earlier research repo keep their original ID with a `(the earlier research repo Kxx)` citation, so provenance is
  always traceable.
- Audit this file at every session close.

Seeded 2026-10-19 from the earlier research repo's `docs/known.md`, rows relevant to this repo's narrower scope
(structure, the 13 carried-over files, and the toolchain facts needed to rebuild them). Rows about
the earlier research repo-only work (resource D shim, remaster roadmap, PCSX2 patches) were left out — they don't
apply here.

| N65 | The menu's `CGt2dScene` instance (`D_01E903E0`) is a member of `CB3MenuPageManager` (`D_01E90240`) at `+0x1A0` | L | Evidence so far: `D_01E903E0 - D_01E90240 = 0x1A0`, and `0x1A0 + 0x34` (our reconstructed `sizeof(CGt2dScene)`) lands exactly on the manager's `field_1D4` (`AddComponent`, [C]). Taking the address as that member reproduces retail's codegen in N63, but so does a plain cast, so that isn't proof. Settled by: a retail function that reaches the scene by adding `0x1A0` to a manager pointer that isn't constant (a `this`-relative access inside a `CB3MenuPageManager` method). `CB3MenuPageManager::SetMenuPage`/`Render`/`Update` are the likely places to look. |
| N70 | Retail's signed byte table at `0x4E1D20` (4 entries `0x0E, 0x16, 0x14, 0x15`, read `$gp`-relative as `0x4E1D1A + type` for types 6-9) is **`kan8HeadingAwardIconImages`** | L | Name from the E-build maps (`research/E_dnat_symbols.csv` etc.: `.sdata`, `B3HeadingComponent.cpp`, but 3 bytes there, vs. the 4 types that index it here). The values index the same texture table (`D_01EA46B0`) the other cases use. Settled by: the E build's `CB3HeadingComponent::Prepare(EHeadingType)` (`0x2D9300` in DNAT) reading that symbol at the same point in the same switch; or a 4th entry/award type in a later build's table. Also unresolved: which object owns retail's `0x4E1D18`-`0x4E1D24` `.sdata` (split into `game/unit_0030E340`'s data today). |
| N72 | `CB3HeadingComponent::SetHeadingString(const unsigned short*)` is the text-fit + `func_003824D0` block, inlined | L | Name: E-build B3O0PCH map (out of line there, 0x110 bytes); absent from DNAT and retail as a function. Retail `Update` has two copies of exactly Target #31's fit code (from `GetString(field_24)` and from `field_20`), and only an inline helper reproduces their stack-slot order (N71). Settled by: the B3O0PCH binary's `SetHeadingString` body, or a DNAT caller that inlines it the same way. `Prepare(text, type)`/`Prepare(id, type)` (N63/N66) may use it too; they match written out, so they were left as they are. |
| N80 (**settled 2026-10-21 by N84**: matched and wired in) | `CB3RotatingImage2dObject::_Render` (`0x31A300`, 0x264) draft (`src/.../B3HeadingComponent_0031A300.cpp`, **not wired in**): 153/153 instructions, identical except one register swap (`$f3`/`$f4` for `space.field_0.x`/`half.x` before the centre add, 4 instructions) | L | Established by single-change variants: (a) N79's inline depth; (b) `CreateRotationMatrix2x3` sets `field_8` before `field_0` (`c3`: the store order then matches exactly); (c) `TransformPoint` as `r = m.field_10; r += m.field_0 * x; r += m.field_8 * y; return r;` (its return-value copy and low temporaries match retail's slot map); (d) N82's accessors; (e) corner/centre/UV statement order from a 360-way permutation search plus follow-ups (best `0C123`, UVs last). Not tried yet: other spellings of the centre (e.g. through a `CGt2dObjectSpace` helper), and a search over declaration order of every local together. Settled by: a variant with 0 differing instructions, then the relink. |
| N81 | `CHeadingBar2dObject::_Render` (`0x31B130`, 0x504) draft (`src/.../B3HeadingComponent_0031B130.cpp`, **not wired in**): 325 vs 321 instructions | L | The structure is recovered and matches retail's slot map for all 8-byte temporaries (source order of `scale`, `c`, `b`, `a`, the zero offset, `d`, `e` read off retail's descending slots): a flat untextured quad coloured `mgr->field_100 * (colour * kHeadingTextBarFillColourScale)`, two 6-vertex Gouraud strips from `field_120`/`field_130`, the textured curve (`krHeadingBarBigCurveEffectiveImageSize`, texture `D_01EA46B0[5]`), a 3-vertex cap. Needs `CGtV4d::operator*(CGtV4d)` (VU0 `vmul.xyzw`, API) and new page-manager fields `field_100`/`field_120`/`field_130` `[C]`. Remaining: the 16-byte-aligned pool reserves more slots than retail (retail: the colour temporary plus 4; ours with the current operator: 10), and one extra `lui` + three extra `lwc1`. `CB3MenuPageManager *const mgr` keeps `&D_01E90240` in `$s0` like retail (a plain pointer gets rematerialized). Settled by: an `operator*` body/parameter form giving retail's slot count, then the instruction-level diff. **an earlier pass:** now 321/321 instructions: batch pointers chained off `colours` (`pos = (CGtV2d *)(colours + 6)`, then `uv`, `quad`), the quad written through `X()`/`Y()` accessors (N82), and the fill colour as its own statement (`CGtV4d fill; fill = space.field_10 * kHeadingTextBarFillColourScale;`), which also gives retail's VU register numbering. Only the frame differs (draft 0x270 vs 0x1E0, every stack offset shifted); a 40-way grid of `CGtV4d::operator*` bodies/parameter forms x colour-statement spellings reaches 0x1A0/0x1C0/0x1F0/0x200/... but never 0x1E0. **Lead from the the earlier research repo survey:** with a user-declared `CGtV4d(const CGtV4d& o) { q = o.q; }` (the API lists a copy ctor, O0 44 B / DNAT 16 B) the frame becomes exactly retail's 0x1E0, but both `RenderTriStripFlatTex` calls then copy `space.field_10` into a 16-byte temp (`lq`/`sq`, +4 instructions) where retail passes `&space.field_10`; a `const` by-value parameter doesn't stop that. the earlier research repo's docs/matching-patterns.md #22 (copy ctor forces a copy per by-value call; use `const CGtV4d` and no copy ctor) points the same way, so the frame must come from somewhere else. Next: the E-build DNAT out-of-line `CGtV4d` bodies (`operator*(CGtV4d)` at E ELF 0x1987D0: `lqc2 vf1,0(a2); lqc2 vf2,0(a1); vmul.xyzw vf1,vf2,vf1; jr ra; sqc2 vf1,0(a0)`) and the copy ctor's 16 bytes. **an earlier pass (Revenge `types.h` lead, negative):** Revenge declares `CGtV4d(u_long128)` (`__Q29GtMathPs26CGtV4dUx`), a copy constructor, axis-type and `(float,float,float,float)` constructors, but keeps no out-of-line bodies. Variants (`build/variants/run_ux.sh`): adding `CGtV4d(u_long128)` and returning `CGtV4d(t)` (or `return t;`) from `operator*` -- either or both operators, body or mem-initializer -- gives 321 instructions at frame 0x200; adding the copy constructor as well gives 325 at 0x1C0; the copy constructor alone 325 at 0x1E0 (as before). None reaches 321 at 0x1E0. Draft frame after the N89 renames is unchanged (0x270). |

| ID | What | Artefact |
|---|---|---|
| N13 (proven) | `orig/SLUS_210.50` extracted here from `<local archive>\Retail\Burnout 3 - Takedown PS2 (USA).iso` has SHA-1 `332be40d6081b8b5055a6ea01194ad6ff662a863` — matching the earlier research repo's canonical build target exactly | `tools/iso_extract.py` + `sha1sum`, re-derived independently in this repo, not copied |
| N14 (proven) | `splat create_config` on that ELF independently reproduces the documented header facts (`entry=0x100008`, `vaddr=0x100000`, `filesz=0x3E2680`, `gp_value=0x4E8670`) — confirms N1-adjacent facts from a second, independent tool run | `tools/elfinfo.py` + splat's own ELF reader, this repo, 2026-10-19 |
| N15 (proven) | Importing the earlier research repo's `config/symbol_addrs.txt`, `config/symbol_addrs_b3names.txt`, `assembly/splat/b3.yaml` (as `config/b3_retail.yaml`) and `build/undefined_syms_auto.txt` (98 absolute-value constants + 8 more we added the same way) resolves every undefined reference across all 1,223 assembled pieces — the original N-H4 jump-table/rodata blocker is gone | `mips-linux-gnu-ld` full link, zero errors, 2026-10-20; `build/full_build.sh` |
| N17 (proven) | `Gt2dObject.cpp`'s two functions (`SetPos`, `Prepare`, 160 bytes) compile **100% byte-identical** to retail (VRAM `0x1D5E00`–`0x1D5EA0`) through the real `mwccps2.exe` 3.0.1b119 running under Wine in this repo | `build/verify_match.py build/mw/Gt2dObject.o 0x1D5E00 0x1D5EA0 build/SLUS_210.50.rom` → exact match, 2026-10-20 |
| N21 (proven) | All 10 of the earlier research repo's `"linked": true` local-fork units (`Gt2dObject`, `Gt2dObjectBox`, and the 8 `B3*`/`GtVideoDecoderPS2` files) compile with the real compiler and, once substituted for their asm counterparts **and relinked** into the full binary, produce **byte-for-byte the same rom** as the all-asm build (identical SHA-1 `6679042a…` before and after substitution) — i.e. all 10 are proven matches, not just "compiles without error" | `build/wire_in_gt2d.sh`, `build/wire_in_b3.sh`, 2026-10-20: both produce rom SHA-1 `6679042a8b24992605621d325ce7917578e10730`, identical to the pre-substitution build |
| N22 (proven, explains N18) | The earlier "9-byte diff" in `Gt2dObjectBox.cpp` (N18) was an artefact of comparing an **unlinked** object's raw bytes: that function contains real relocations (`R_MIPS_26` calls to `Gt2dRenderer::RenderSpritesUntex`/`CGt2dObject::Prepare`, an `R_MIPS_HI16`/`LO16` pair for a vtable-thunk address) that are placeholder zeros until linked. Once linked (N21), it matches exactly — there was no real bug | `mips-linux-gnu-objdump -r build/mw/Gt2dObjectBox.o` showing the relocations, cross-checked against N21's result, 2026-10-20 |

## 3b. Believed / unresolved from this repo's own compiler runs

| ID | Belief | Grade | Experiment |
|---|---|---|---|
| N20 (refined 2026-10-20; **settled 2026-10-21 by N85**: data-section alignment) | With the size gap fixed (N-H5), the full rebuild is still content-different from retail: 35,825 of 4,073,088 bytes (0.88%), unaffected by wiring in all 10 proven units (N21) | G | **Localized** (`tools/map_diffs_to_units.py`): 403 of ~1,042 subsegments carry at least one differing byte; the top 40 units (3.8% of them) hold 24,600/35,825 bytes (**68.7%** — concentrated, not uniform noise). `game/unit_0015F270` alone (VRAM `0x15F270`-`0x1A4150`, the `CB3EffectsManager`/particle-effects region bordering RenderWare Audio) holds 7,240 bytes (20% of the total) across 3,722 tiny runs. By category diffs track code-size share roughly proportionally (`game` 91.5%, `sce` 2.6%, `rw` 1.7%, `rwa` 1.5%, rest smaller) -- not a library-vs-game systemic bug. Spot-checking `unit_0015F270` (`tools/inspect_unit_diff.py`) shows a recurring 1-byte mismatch in an `addiu $v0,$v0,0x26xx` immediate inside a repeated 3-instruction sequence (mine `0x2680`, retail `0x2660`) -- consistent with a `$gp`-relative/mid-array addressing ambiguity in spimdisasm's heuristics, the same class as the `ofs_*` cases resolved for linking (N15) but scattered across far more addresses than the 8 already enumerated. Not yet fixed -- this is the real remaining frontier, not a quick patch. |
| N24 | `CGt2dFont::GetStringStartAndEnd` is implemented (was a stub declaration) and behaviorally reasoned from the disassembly: word-wrap text measurement, breaking at the last space seen, with a `flag`-gated mid-word hard break and special newline handling. Compiles and **links cleanly end to end** (lit4-retargeting from N23 applies to its own literal too, second confirmation it generalizes) | L | **Iterated 2026-10-20, converged but not closed.** Started at 684 bytes vs. retail's 648 (36-byte gap); narrowed to **680 bytes (32-byte gap)**, with the **first 0x68 (104) bytes byte-for-byte identical** to retail, by: (1) passing `*s` directly to the first `UnkInline_FindGlyph` call instead of through a cached local (removed a redundant `andi 0xffff` mask, -4 bytes); (2) reordering `foundBreak`'s initialization before the separator-skip loop (matched retail's instruction order for that span, no size change). Tried and **reverted** three further restructurings that each made it worse, not better: a `while(*s)`-shaped main loop (692 bytes), the same with the dispatch as a `switch` (688 bytes), and an explicit `goto`-based layout mirroring retail's exact block shape including its initial unconditional jump to the loop's bottom test (688 bytes, and reintroduced the redundant-mask problem). None reproduced retail's specific `b <bottom-test>` instruction at the loop entry (present in retail at every attempt's equivalent point, absent in all of mine) or its exact constant-load order (retail: 10, 1, 32; every attempt of mine: some other order) — the remaining 32-byte/structural gap is concentrated in *why CodeWarrior chooses that one specific rotation*, not in the measured byte count. 39.5% raw byte overlap in the (longer) compiled range; closing the rest needs either more idiom variations than a human can efficiently search by hand, or a permuter-equivalent tool (none built this session). |
| N19 / N23 (lit4 part resolved 2026-10-20) | `Gt2dFont.cpp` can't yet be wired in and relink-verified like the other 10 | L | Its address range (`0x1D7A70`-`0x1D8260`, K30) is its own clean, isolated subsegment, renamed from `game/unit_001D7A70` to `b3/GameShared/GameClasses/Graphical/2d/Gt2dFont` in `config/b3_retail.yaml`. Two blockers found: **(1) `.lit4` -- fixed**, see the writeup below. **(2) still open**: `Gt2dFont.cpp` is missing a method other code calls directly, `GetStringStartAndEnd__9CGt2dFontCFPCUsfPPCUsPPCUsb` (confirmed `undefined reference` at three call sites: `unit_002F8C50`, `unit_002F9690`, `unit_0032EE00`) -- the file is genuinely partial, like `Gt2dObjectImage`/`Gt2dScene`. With the `.lit4` fix applied, relinking `Gt2dFont.cpp` in now fails with **only** that one missing-method error (plus a handful of pre-existing, unrelated `$gp` small-data overflow warnings also seen when wiring in `Gt2dObjectImage`/`Gt2dScene` earlier -- not caused by this change, not yet investigated) -- confirming the `.lit4` blocker is gone. |
| N90 (2026-10-21) | **Nahelam's retail function names checked against code** (`tools/check_nahelam_names.py` -> `research/nahelam_check.csv`): of 1,146 in-image rows, 636 agree with `research/retail_names.csv`; 111 conflict (our high/medium name differs, or the name is ours at another address); 154 rejected (not a splat function start, or DNAT size far off); 58 have no DNAT name; 68 weak; **119 candidates** (function start, DNAT size within 25%/16 bytes, a same-source-file named neighbour, no conflict). Calibration: `0x319C80`, which Nahelam names `Prepare(const unsigned short*, EHeadingType)`, is flagged as a conflict, correctly: that overload is `0x319A90` (high) and `0x319C80` is `Prepare(EGlobalStrings, EHeadingType)` (N66). The wiki's PAL->US shifts of `-0x428`/`-0x480`/`-0x2F0` give only rejects/conflicts. Nothing has been merged into the name tables. | L | Per candidate: merging it at medium confidence and regenerating names **Settled for the 119 candidates:** all merged into `research/retail_names.csv` at medium confidence (`check_nahelam_names.py --merge`, method `nahelam+check_nahelam_names (N90)`, DNAT spelling) -> 112 new symbols; 7 stay comments (4 placeholders used by upstream's or our C, 2 already named by upstream, 1 with no symbol form). See N92. |
| N94 (2026-10-21) | **`GtMathPs2::Interpolate(CGtV4d, CGtV4d, float)` (`a + (b - a) * t` through ACC, b/t/result via GPRs with `qmtc2.ni`/`qmfc2.ni`) can't be reproduced inlined yet.** It blocks the drafts `func_0031BD60` (`B3HelpBarComponent_0031BD60.cpp`, 118/120) and `func_0031BF40` (`_0031BF40.cpp`, 175/177): everything else in both matches, including the frames (0x90/0xF0) and stack slots once the result is returned through `CGtV4d(u_long128)` (Revenge declares that ctor; a named `CGtV4d` result adds a 16-byte slot) and the by-value colour copies are explicit locals assigned with `operator=`. MWCC turns `lq b; qmtc2.ni b` into one `lqc2`, and `qmfc2.ni r; sq r` into `sqc2`: tried `volatile`; `#pragma peephole off` scoped around the inline definition (no effect: it doesn't reach inlined code), file-wide (keeps the moves but breaks every address fold, +18 instructions); an `"m"` operand with the `lq` inside the asm; `b` as an in/out `"+r"` operand. **Also observed:** where `Interpolate` is defined in `GtMathPs2.h` changes other functions' code -- defined after `CGtV4d::SetA` it changed `CB3HeadingComponent::Update`'s `qmtc2` scheduling (its `.lit4` retargets then failed); defined before `SetA` the heading's code is unchanged (and these two drafts lose one instruction each). Cf. matching-patterns v13. **an earlier pass, mechanism pinned down (probes `build/variants/ipt*.cpp`):** MWCC models `qmtc2`/`qmfc2` written `.ni` or unsuffixed (any case) as known copies: it folds `lq r; qmtc2 r,vfN` into `lqc2 vfN` and stores a `qmfc2`'d result with `sqc2` from the VU register, and it renames an operand the asm overwrites (so `a` and `b` never share `$vf1` as in retail) -- with `"+j"`, input-only `"j"`, hard-coded `$vf1`/`$vf2`, in/out `"+r"` b, a separate statement per move, or by-reference parameters. Written `.i`, each move is opaque and the output is **exactly retail's shape** (`lqc2 $vf1,0x60(...)`, `lq v0,0x30(...)`, `qmtc2 v0,$vf1`, `qmfc2 v0,$vf1`, `sq v0`), but `.i` sets bit 0 and retail's moves are `.ni` (bit 0 clear), so not byte-exact. `#pragma peephole off` around the inline definition has no effect; around the calling function it stops the folds but bounces every value (a too) through GPRs. Not a compiler-build difference: 3.0.1b103 (DNAT's) folds the same forms, while DNAT's own inlined copies (e.g. 0x1737E4) show retail's unfolded shape -- so the original header used a form not found yet. Loading `a` inside the asm (`lqc2 $vf1, 0(%1)` on `&a`) keeps b's move unfolded, but needs a by-reference `a` (frame loses the two reserved 16-byte slots) or makes a real copy of a by-value `a`. | L | An `Interpolate` body/operand form that keeps the GPR moves inlined; then the two drafts' remaining diffs |
| N95 (2026-10-21) | **`CB3HelpBarComponent::Update(float)` draft** (`B3HelpBarComponent_0031B860.cpp`, not wired in): 303/318 instructions, frame 0x80 vs 0x70. Structure recovered: `if (!field_14) return;` then states 2-4 (slide the active objects to `432 - h/2 - lift`, `lift = 32 * D_01E90420` computed once: a precomputed product, not fused into `msub`) and 5 (per-side pop-in from `field_54[i]` with the 0.8/0.4 `.lit4` literals, `d *= -0.5f` in place via the API's `CGtV2d::operator*=(float)`; the two bound slides to `field_64`/`field_68`; text mode slides with lift 32), each state ending in its own copy of the text-mode check (re-`Prepare` on a selection change, else scroll by `dt`), as retail has two copies. The y is set through the API's `CGtV2d::SetY` on a stack copy. **an earlier pass:** 316/318, frame 0x70 (matches), 134 instructions in changed runs: one shared position local in states 2-4 (retail reuses one slot and `&pos.y` for all four slides) and `d` declared outside the pop-in loop gave the frame; `if (elapsed > 0.4f)` gives retail's block layout. Left: the bound slides (retail compares and computes from the register x, ours reloads it), the slide's `32 * t` register, and the stack-slot order -- retail's second-bound and text-slide copies sit below the pop-in temporaries (0x18/0x20), ours just below the first bound's (0x50); MWCC's output is identical whatever scope those locals are declared in, so the order comes from something else (temporaries vs named locals?). **an earlier pass:** now **318/318 instructions**, frame 0x70, 40 in changed runs, from N99's rules. Each bound slide's `else` clears `field_6C` before `SetX`. Every write-back to an object is `obj->mObjectSpace.mTopLeftPos.Set(pos.x, pos.y)`, as is the state-5 text slide's. An aggregate `obj->pos = pos` keeps the copy in memory, so x was reloaded. Left: the stack-slot order (retail's second-bound and text-slide copies sit below the operator results: the slot of an inline function's return-value local, not of a named local or a helper's local; which inline is unknown); the state 2-4 slide's `32 * t` register; one `mul.s` operand order. **an earlier pass:** retail's second-bound (0x18) and text-slide (0x20) copies are in N99's bottom region, below the three loop results, i.e. used return values of inline functions called after the loop, text first. The 0x50 slot is reserved but never touched. Tried in full context: both as returned-local helpers (K1-K3), the text slide's result assigned or `Set` back (K4/K5), a reassigned named copy from a by-value `Pos()` (probe 14); none reproduces the slot map without extra copies. ### `.lit4` retargeting technique (built 2026-10-20, resolves the N23 `.lit4` blocker)

**The problem.** CodeWarrior puts every float constant it can't build with `lui` into a private
`.lit4` section inside the compiled object, loaded via an `R_MIPS_LITERAL` relocation at a local
symbol (CodeWarrior names it something like `@214`). The real retail binary pools *all* such literals
across the whole game into one shared `.lit4` region at fixed addresses (imported here as part of the
combined `.data` region, from `assembly/asm/data/lit4...`/`config/symbol_addrs.txt`). A freshly
compiled object's private copy is never pulled into our link (the ld script only requests each
object's `.text`), so once nothing else needs it, the relocation's target symbol is "defined in a
discarded section" and the link fails.

**Why not just discard the private section and move on?** The relocation still needs *some*
resolvable symbol, and MIPS `R_MIPS_LITERAL`/`R_MIPS_GPREL16` relocations are `$gp`-relative — they
must point at the exact real address the original retail instruction's compiled `$gp`+offset load
reads, or the two binaries diverge even though the value is numerically the same.

**The technique** (`tools/lit4_retarget.py`, reimplemented fresh from the one-paragraph description
in `docs/layout.md`'s "Small data and the `.lit4` pool" section, not from the earlier research repo's
`tools/litfix.py` code):
1. For each `R_MIPS_LITERAL` relocation in the object, find its containing function and look up
   that function's known retail VRAM (`config/symbol_addrs_b3names.txt`).
2. Read the **real retail instruction's own bytes** at that exact offset, decode its 16-bit `$gp`
   immediate, and compute the absolute pool address it actually loads from
   (`GP_VALUE + signed_immediate`). This is deterministic — no "most likely" guessing — because we
   already know the retail bytes for any function we're wiring in to verify.
3. Sanity-check: the 4 bytes at that computed address in the retail rom must equal the object's own
   private `.lit4` value. (For `Gt2dFont.cpp`'s one literal, `-FLT_MAX` / `0xFFFF7FFF`, two addresses
   in the shared pool hold that value — `0x4E0D48` and `0x4E111C` — and the retail-instruction-decode
   step picked `0x4E111C` unambiguously, the one the real binary actually used for *this* function.)
4. Rename the relocation's symbol (`objcopy --redefine-sym`) to a new name encoding the target
   address (`lit4_004E111C`), then **promote it local→global→weak**
   (`--globalize-symbol`+`--weaken-symbol`). A plain rename isn't enough: a *local* symbol always
   wins within its own object no matter what the linker is told elsewhere, so the reference has to
   stop being local before an external definition can take over. The object's now-orphaned private
   `.lit4` section is harmless left in place — the ld script never asks for it.
5. Emit `lit4_<ADDR> = 0x<ADDR>;` into a small defs file (`build/lit4_retarget_defs.txt`, same
   generated-facts category as `build/undefined_syms_auto.txt`), passed to the final `ld` invocation
   alongside the other `--defsym`-style facts.

Verified: relinking `Gt2dFont.cpp` with this applied clears the `.lit4` error completely, leaving only
the expected `GetStringStartAndEnd` missing-method error — confirmed with `tools/wire_in_font.sh`.

| N25 (proven) | The `$gp` small-data overflow warnings seen when wiring in `Gt2dFont.cpp`/`Gt2dObjectImage.cpp`/`Gt2dScene.cpp` have **no independent structural or compiler-flag cause** — they are a direct, mechanical consequence of N24's unresolved size gap, not a separate problem | The overflowing symbols (`D_004E06E4`, `D_004E069C`, `D_004E08A8`, `D_004E0950`, `D_004E06A8`) are **not** pinned absolute addresses in `config/symbol_addrs.txt`/`symbol_addrs_b3names.txt` — they are `dlabel`s auto-named by splat from VRAM address, defined inside `assembly/asm/data/lit4.sdata.s`, whose *actual linked position* is wherever the linker places the `.sdata` section after everything earlier in link order. `$gp` itself (`0x4E8670`) is a fixed absolute constant matching retail's true, unshifted layout. `Gt2dFont.cpp` compiling 32 bytes larger than retail (N24) shifts everything after it — including the whole shared `.sdata`/`.sbss` region — 32 bytes later than where `$gp` "expects" it, tipping a few symbols already close to the `$gp` ±32 KB (`R_MIPS_GPREL16`) boundary over the edge. Confirmed no other contributing cause: the all-asm build (before any of the three partial files are wired in) links with zero such warnings (N15); the warnings appear only once a wired-in object's size doesn't match its retail slot exactly. |

Both are reusable idioms for future functions with the same shapes (zeroing an 8-byte-aligned pair, or writing the same sentinel value to fields of different widths). | — | `tools/compile_trackrenderer.sh`, iterated 2026-10-20 |

The sibling classes `CB3HSelectComponent::Update()` etc. (`assembly/asm/game/unit_0030B560.s` and others) share a real `if (D_01E90424 == 5) { ... }` guard, comparing a global against the literal `5` before doing their real per-frame work. For `CB3VListComponent`, the guard is the **entire** function body (`if (D_01E90424 == 5) return;` — functionally equivalent to a no-op either way, since there's nothing after it). Several naive translations were tried first and rejected because they reproduced only a subset of retail's 5 instructions: a plain `if (global == 5) {}` (no `volatile`) eliminates everything (0 instructions); the same with `D_01E90424` marked `volatile` keeps only the global's read (1 instruction, wrong register, no `5`); an explicitly inlined empty-function call with the global and `5` as arguments also collapses to a single volatile read. Only `if (D_01E90424 == 5) return;` (plain, non-volatile global) reproduced the full 5-instruction shape (`lui`/`lw`/`addiu`/`jr`/`nop`) with the exact same registers as retail (`$a0` for the global's value, `$v1` reused for the literal `5`) — evidently CodeWarrior's -O4 generates the full comparison-and-branch skeleton for an early-return form, then later folds away just the (duly-provably-redundant) branch instruction itself without retroactively cleaning up the now-dead operand loads that fed it; an empty-block `if` or an already-constant-folded case never generates that skeleton in the first place, so nothing is left to leave behind. Needed the N51 placeholder-padding idiom again (a padded `UnkStruct_D_01E90424` wrapper) to get the correct 2-instruction absolute addressing for the extern instead of a 1-instruction `R_MIPS_GPREL16` form. **General idiom for this ledger**: when a function's disassembly looks like "orphaned" operand-loads with no comparison/branch/use, check whether an early-return `if` (not an empty-block `if`) reproduces it — CodeWarrior's branch-folding pass seems to run after dead-load elimination specifically for this control-flow shape. | `tools/wire_in_full_check.sh`, 2026-10-21: rom SHA-1 `6679042a8b24992605621d325ce7917578e10730`; compiled object instruction-for-instruction identical to retail, including exact register allocation |

| N61 | **MWCC inline-asm facts, from this function's dead-end asm attempt** (all confirmed in isolated test compiles, 2026-10-21). Use these if a future function really needs a whole-function `asm volatile` block that makes calls. (a) A bare `jal MangledName` in asm text fails with "undefined label" **unless that exact linkage name has its own `extern "C"` declaration** in the translation unit. A normal C++ method declaration isn't enough, even one with a real implementation elsewhere in the repo (`Release__20CB3MenuComponentBaseFv`, tested alone). With `extern "C" void Release__20CB3MenuComponentBaseFv();` it compiles to a correct `R_MIPS_26` relocation. (b) `%hi(SYM)`/`%lo(SYM)` is rejected ("illegal operand") with or without `extern "C"`. (c) **Any explicit `$sp` adjustment in the asm text** (with or without `$ra`) makes the compiler wrap the statement in its *own* second frame, nested around the hand-written one. (d) The compiler **scans the asm text for callee-saved registers and saves/restores them in its own prologue whether or not they're in the clobber list** (it saved `$s0` without a clobber, and skipped a clobbered `$s2` the text never used). Its prologue always comes as one block *before* the asm text, so a retail delay-slot register save can't be reproduced this way. That's why `Release()` had to go back to plain C++. **Corrects a false lead** from the cut-off session's uncommitted code comment ("a dummy C++ call makes the symbol resolvable"). Retested: dummy calls, real definitions compiled first, `-O0`, and call ordering all fail. The original test also had a wrong mangled-name length (`19` for a 21-character class name). | isolated compiles via the scratch `test_asm_destruct.cpp` (deleted afterward), 2026-10-21 |

| N99 (proven by probes, 2026-10-21) | **MWCC -O4 stack-slot and copy rules** (probe files `build/variants/slots*.cpp`, scratch). (1) A function's **named locals get the highest slots, in declaration order** (earlier = higher), wherever they are declared: nested blocks, other `case`s, after the code that runs first (S1, S4, N3); moving a declaration changes the slot, moving only its initialisation doesn't. (2) Below them come the caller-side temporaries that receive by-value returns (the `ld`/`sd` copies), then **inlined functions' locals**: a helper's named locals in the helper's definition order (HA above HB whatever the call order: M1-M3), and below those the operators' own result locals (`CGtV2d r; ... return r;`). (3) **A copied `CGtV2d` stays in registers only if every write-back is field-wise**: with an aggregate `obj->pos = pos`, a branch that writes the copy (`pos.SetX(...)` in an `else`) makes the other branch reload x from the stack slot (C9/C10 vs C12); `obj->pos.Set(pos.x, pos.y)` keeps x and y in registers (K4 / Update). A user-declared `CGtV2d::operator=` does the same, but it also stops `field = CGtV2d(a, b)` going through a stack temporary, which breaks 5 proven units (`B3HeadingComponent_00319490/_00319A90/_00319C80/_0031A9C0`, `B3HelpBarComponent_0031C210`), so retail's header has none (tested and reverted). (4) Statement order inside a branch changes allocation: clearing a flag before the `SetX` in Update's clamp. **an earlier pass, refined (probes `slots8`-`slots14`):** (2) is really two regions below the caller temporaries. First, non-returned locals of inlined functions (free helpers or member functions alike), in **call order** (R1/R2, Run/Run2: probe 7's apparent fixed order was a misreading). Second, at the bottom, **returned locals whose value the caller uses** (an operator's `r`, a helper's `return r;`, a returned by-value parameter), again in call order (V1/V2, W1); an ignored return value leaves the local in the first region (K1). Accessor forms (`Pos()` by value/reference, `SetPos`) put a copy in the named region (P*). | N114 (proven, 2026-10-21) | **How `CGtV4d` copies compile (isolated experiment, `build/variants/V4X.cpp`).** With the current header: a by-value argument taken straight from memory is **not copied** (its address is passed); a **local copy** (`CGtV4d c = src`) uses the implicit copy constructor, **lane by lane** (`lwc1`/`swc1` x4); `operator=` into an existing object is **one `lq`/`sq`**; `operator*(float)` loads the source **straight into a VU register** (`lqc2`); `SetA()` on a lane-copied local goes stack -> `lqc2` -> `vmulx.w` -> `sqc2`; the four-float constructor is a stack temporary + `lq`/`sq`. **Retail's `PrepareBorders` sequence** (`lqc2` from the source, `qmtc2`, `vmulx.w vf1, vf0, vf2x`, `sqc2` to the argument temporary) = **SetA on a copy whose 128-bit value is moved whole**, which two forms give with no header change: `CGtV4d c(src.mData)` and `CGtV4d c; c = src;` -- then `c.SetA(a)`. A user copy constructor (`mData = o.mData`, E lists `CGtV4d(const CGtV4d&)` [C]) also gives it, but **changes proven units** (`B3HeadingComponent_00319E80`, `B3HelpBarComponent_0031C210` stop linking: their copies' code changes) -- reverted. | V4X compile listings; header unchanged (`build_c_units.sh` -> `MATCHES RETAIL`) |

## 4. Retracted

| ID | Retracted claim | What killed it |
|---|---|---|
| N62 (retracts N55's identity, 2026-10-21) | ~~`func_0030D3B0` (Target #25) is `CB3MenuPageManager::Construct()`~~, and the 0x0-0x177 field layout `B3MenuPageManager.h` carried from it | Its only caller (`d5/frontend_flow.s`, `0x1319C8`) passes `this = &D_01E900B0`, but the page manager instance is `D_01E90240` (proven: `CB3MenuComponentBase::Construct` calls `AddComponent` on it). That's 0x190 bytes later, and the function never writes past `+0x177`, so it initializes a *different* object that ends before the manager starts. That 0x0-0x177 layout also conflicted with `CB3HeadingComponent::Prepare`/`Update` reading 16-byte values at manager `+0x40`/`+0x50`, which is how this surfaced. Fixed: the function is now `extern "C" void func_0030D3B0(UnkStruct_D_01E900B0*)` (placeholder name, C linkage, so its `undefined_syms_auto.txt` alias was removed), the layout moved into a comment in its own file, and `B3MenuPageManager.h` keeps only evidenced members. The bytes are still proven, re-verified byte-identical after the rename (rom SHA-1 `6679042a…`). Its file placement among `CB3MenuPageManager`'s methods stays `[L]` (N2 contiguity). Lesson: an `[L]` identity based on "it sits next to class X's methods and looks like a constructor" should at least check what `this` its callers pass. |
| N77 (retracts a `GtMathPs2.h` [D] entry, 2026-10-21) | ~~CGtV2d's scalar multiply is a free operator taking the vector by value, `operator*(const CGtV2d, float)`, "not in the E map (only the member vector * vector operator is)"~~ | The GtMathPs2 API list (from the B3O0PCH map) does have the member `CGtV2d::operator *(float)`, and `func_0031A9C0` matches only with the vector taken by reference (variants `w0` vs `y1`): the by-value form stores and reloads it. Replaced by the member. Its only other claimed user (`Gt2dFont.cpp`, not linked) has no vector * float expression. |
| N88 (retracts N34, 2026-10-21) | ~~`build/SLUS_210.50.rom` with SHA-1 `817cfb41...` is a stale build artifact; the correct baseline is `6679042a...`~~ | Backwards: `817cfb41...` is the **retail** load segment (`tools/elf_container.py extract orig/SLUS_210.50`), and `6679042a...` was our own rebuild, 35,825 bytes off retail (N20). From an earlier pass to 31 `build/SLUS_210.50.rom` held our build, and every "baseline SHA-1" check compared our build against itself. Function-level proofs were still sound for code bytes (C vs the same retail-disassembled asm), but 13 Menu units differed from retail in relocated data addresses until N85. The retail segment now lives at `orig/SLUS_210.50.rom` and `wire_in_full_check.sh` compares against it directly (`MATCHES RETAIL`). |

## 5. Standing hazards (traps), carried from the earlier research repo where still relevant

| ID | Trap | Guard |
|---|---|---|
| N-H1 (the earlier research repo H5) | Upstream (`siddharthakumar-98/Burnout3`) names often come from Burnout 2 or are invented; never evidence here either, even indirectly through anything we read | Only trust the E map, B2/Revenge primary data, and this repo's own verification |
| N-H2 (the earlier research repo H2) | `research/E_dnat_symbols.csv` holds raw map addresses, not aligned ELF addresses | Use `research/names_E_jun8.csv` (aligned) for E addresses |
| N-H3 | The 13 carried-over source files were objdiff-verified **inside the earlier research repo's toolchain**, not this repo's | Resolved for the 5 Gt2d files by N16-N19; the other 7 (now reclassified, see `docs/layout.md`) still untested against this repo's own compiler |
| N-H4 (retracted as a blocker, 2026-10-20) | A coarse ("zero-knowledge") splat split with no real function/data boundaries mis-identifies `.rodata` const data as jump tables, producing implausible "addresses" that fail to link | **Resolved**: importing the earlier research repo's resolved `config/symbol_addrs.txt`/`b3_retail.yaml` (see `docs/provenance.md`) gives real boundaries; this stopped happening (N15) |
| N-H5 (fixed 2026-10-20) | ~~The full 1,223-piece asm-only build links cleanly (N15) but is 96 bytes larger than retail~~ | **Root cause found, not the "missing .end" files**: `config/b3_retail.yaml`'s own comment says library objects (ee-gcc, 8-byte aligned) need their `.text` alignment set per-object by `configure.py`, since `emit_subalign: False` leaves splat's output with no `.align` hints. The assembler defaults every object to GNU AS's blanket 16-byte `.text` alignment regardless; where a library unit's real VRAM start is only 8-aligned (6 of them: `sce/libdma`, `runtime/libm`, `sce/libdbc`, `lg/lgkbm`, `sce/libnet`, `ea/dirtysock`), the linker inserted phantom rounding padding before each. Forcing each object's true alignment (`tools/fix_alignment.py`, reimplemented from the technique in the comment, not copied) via `objcopy --set-section-alignment` closed the gap to **exactly 0 bytes** (`4073088` both sides) |
| N168 (proven, 2026-10-26) | **MWCC's memcpy intrinsic: an undeclared `__memcpy(dst, src, n)` reproduces retail's inlined block copy** (an `lq`/`sq` loop, then an `lh`/`lbu` tail through the advanced pointers). [C] func_00139F20 (0x240) and func_00139790 (0x470) proven with it; declaring `memcpy` yourself gives a `jal` instead [C]. [G] that Criterion's source called `memcpy` for these arrays. A 3-byte tail as `struct { unsigned char b[3]; }` gives retail's load/store order [C]. Open: memberwise copies of mixed int/float structs (func_00138D60 / 0013B820 / 001364B0): retail reuses the previous byte copy's register for the next load. | config/proven_units.txt 0x139F20, 0x139790; src/b3/_unclassified/func_00139F20.cpp; build/vt/hand/a100_* |

| N171 (2026-10-26) | **MWCC b119's register tie-breaks depend on the compiler's memory layout: the source file's byte length and its path's length change the code, under native Windows as well as wibo** [C]. Found by the slot investigator (wibo: CalculateSpeedMatch flips a register rename with a leading comment of N chars); lead check on native Windows (cmd.exe, same flags): the same source gives one of two `.text` forms by pad length (0/50/600/1000 vs 200/400/2000) and by path name length (1 vs 10/40), repeatably for identical inputs; wine gave one form throughout; wibo's pattern differs from native's at the different absolute paths used. So N165's "wibo == native on all proven sources" holds for those paths/contents, not in general [L]. Consequences: (1) a proven unit can flip when its file content or path changes (tu_merge, header edits, renames) -- the gates catch it; (2) some register-only near-misses may have the right source and miss only by layout [L]; matching them by padding/path tricks is NOT allowed (would be a fakematch) without a project decision; nonmatch notes record "LAYOUT-FLIP: matches at pad N" as evidence. | build/vt/hand/lead_sz/ (p*/q* sources, n*=native, w*=wibo, e*=wine objects); build/vt/hand/slot_sm8.txt. **Census (lead, same day): of 163 same-length archived near-misses, 0 match exactly at any of 33 pad lengths (0..2048, wibo) and 156 don't change at all -- the archive's register/schedule misses are real source-shape gaps, not layout flips [C] (build/vt/hand/layout_census.py / .txt).** |

| N175 (2026-10-26) | **GIF/GS packet quads: retail's `pcpyld` is inline asm, and how to shape it.** [C] MWCC b119 has a real `_pcpyld(hi, lo)` builtin (an MMI/VU intrinsic table in mwccps2.exe; needs `#pragma vu0_mmi_reg_binding on`), but it takes u_long128 arguments and adds `pcpyld x,zero,x` per 64-bit half, which retail never has; unions/struct punning go through the stack (`sd; sd; lq`); `(u_long128)x << 64` is an illegal data size. So retail's pcpyld is inline asm and the N134 one-line helper (30 proven sources, the lifter) is faithful [L]; RenderWare 3.5 sky2 rwcore.h's Metrowerks `MAKE128(RES, MSB, LSB)` is such an asm macro, likely what Criterion's code used [L]. Shapes [C]: packet pointer `u_long128 *p = farFogPlane.field_0;`, `"=r"(*p++)` per quad, `farFogPlane.field_0 = p;` once; constant quads low half first (`%0, %2, %1`), global-loaded low halves register-first (`%0, %1, %2`); shared halves/`lui`s come from plain literals (compiler CSE); zero halves as an `unsigned long z;` "r" operand without casts, declared after the pointer, `z = 0;` after the first quad. Proven with them: SetRenderstates of CB3DirectionalBlur / CB3RadialBlur, B3Im3d SetLineAntialiasing / SetAlphaTestThreshold (+1). | N183 (proven, 2026-10-26) | **b119's register colouring rules** [C] (regrot investigator; probes build/vt/regrot/p*.cpp, index notes.txt). Values are numbered in creation order; a simplify pass in that order pushes every value of interference degree < K, repeatedly; values pop in reverse, each taking the lowest free register -- so under low pressure a **later-numbered value gets a lower s-register**, and a high-degree value (overlapping >= 4 later temporaries) is deferred and coloured first (s0). **Declaration order sets the number only for "kept" locals** -- passed bare as a call argument, or defined more than once; a local defined once and used only in expressions is folded into a temporary numbered at its definition (declaration order irrelevant); temporaries are numbered after all kept locals. A re-assigned local keeps its declaration number for its first value only; later assignments get late numbers (low registers). Parameters are never split; a same-type copy of a parameter merges into it. `const` / `register` / helper inlines: no effect. Signed `a + b` operands get reordered; `(unsigned)a + (unsigned)b` keeps source order and the result reuses a's register. [L] Same allocator as the reconstructed PPC MWCC 1.2.5 `Coloring.c` (github inspiredrobot/mwcc). Proven by matches: EvaluateGameCounts, CheckForNewHull, func_003FBDB0, func_003FB8B0; Apply__23CB3SoundEsmGraphOutputs improved 6/7 -> 2. | build/vt/regrot/ |

Inert in b119 (no effect anywhere): opt_removeemptyloops, opt_moveinvariantsinaddressexpr, opt_createvectortemps, opt_(de/re)composeaggregates, opt_strength_reduction, opt_reverseloops off, opt_marknonregtemps, opt_pointer_analysis, maxtempforblkmv 1/2/8, padloop on, tailcall, fast_fptosi, and others listed in sweep.log. `schedule off`, `peephole off`, `opt_common_subs off` are net very harmful (200+ length breaks). | N186 (proven, 2026-10-26) | **b119's frame layout** [C] (stack investigator; probes build/vt/stack/p1-p13.cpp, index notes.txt). Slots are assigned top-down, sorted stably by **alignment class**: small (<= 4 bytes, first-fit, later ones fill earlier gaps), then 8-byte (long; aggregates/arrays of 5..15 bytes, e.g. char[12]), then 16-byte (u_long128, CGtV4d, aggregates of 16+ bytes). Within a class, list order: **named locals in declaration order, then the function's own temporaries in statement order (outermost first), then inlined bodies in expansion order** (a helper's locals sit below the caller's temps, even later statements'). Operator chains: `T r = chain;` / `*o = chain;` put the outer result locals first; a chain passed as an argument or `return chain;` puts the inner ones first. **No slot reuse** across blocks, arms or cases; unused locals get no slot. Dead slots kept for: a named copy passed by value to an out-of-line call, an inline helper's by-value parameter, a lane read X()/W() (N172); a plain copy whose address is never taken is dropped. N140 pads are ordinary small address-taken locals (they grow the frame only when nothing else is in that class and 16-aligned locals sit below) -- size a pad to choose its class. Frame areas from sp+0, each rounded to 16: 9th+ call arguments (8 bytes each), saved FPRs (f20 +0, f21 +4), saved GPRs (16 bytes each, s0 lowest), ra, locals. Proven by 11 matches (GetAlignPos, DeformFront/RearBumper, func_001D6970, func_0033D380, func_001D6AD0, func_0033D4E0, UpdateWibbleAnimationIn, CB3SoundTrafficVehicle::Activate, ...); 5 archives improved. | build/vt/stack/ |

| N188 (2026-10-26) | **The VU0 move folds (N94) are four use-def peephole rules present since b87** [C] (fold investigator; build/vt/fold/notes.txt). b119/b119p: `qmfc2 r`->`sq r` becomes `sqc2` (0x558940); `sqc2` of a qmtc2-filled register becomes `sq` (0x558a10); a `qmfc2`->`qmtc2` round trip is removed or becomes `vmove` (0x558b10/0x558bc0); `lq r`->`qmtc2 r` becomes `lqc2` (0x558c90, the only one gated by VU0 being enabled: `#pragma enable_vu0_registers off` stops just it). All one basic block. `.ni` is the plain opcode (display name only); `.i` is a separate opcode the rules ignore. History: b38-b52 / 3.0.3 none; b44-b75 only the sq forward; b87+ (b95, b103, b119, b145...b210) all four -- so retail's unfolded shape is not an older-compiler effect. **No patch** [C]: disabling any rule changes proven units (36 / 15 / 28 of 3251 sources). What stops the folds: `.i` moves; the VU register touched or the GPR reused in between; a later `vcallms` (lqc2 only); **an `asm(".set noreorder")` ... `asm(".set reorder")` region** (keeps qmfc2+sq and round trips, still allows lqc2); scoped `#pragma peephole off` (everything). Not: volatile, "memory" clobbers, separate statements, by-value/const-ref inline parameters, opt_* pragmas. [L] Census of retail's unproven asm: 380 unforwarded qmfc2->sq pairs, 391 kept round trips, 93 dead qmfc2 kept, lq->qmtc2 pairs almost always unfolded -- the behaviour of code in `.set noreorder` regions, not of `.i` or volatile: Criterion's GtMathPs2 asm bodies were probably written inside noreorder regions. Matched: BuildClippedEdgeList 0x2B87E0 (noreorder region around the DotProduct result + plane copied to a u_long128 local before the loop), GetAvgRightwardsVector / GetAvgLeftwardsVector (result move `qmfc2.i`); GetClosestBitToPoint 20 -> 8 (VU numbering left). Next: a header-wide noreorder experiment (headsafe-gated). | build/vt/fold/ |

| N191 (2026-10-26) | **VU0 register numbering under b119p** [C] (vunum; build/vt/vunum/notes.txt, probes and tools). A VU value is numbered when the asm that defines it is created, in the final order after inlining; later values get lower registers; `"j"` input loads are numbered at the asm that uses them; hoisted values keep their creation number; no degree deferral up to 23 (select takes the lowest free); a `$vfN` hard-coded inside a loop excludes N for loop-wide values. **No kept-local rule** (N183 is GPR-only): declaration order, re-definition and class-object operands change nothing. Identical under b87, b103, b119p and R3.7; the vu0_* and cse_hard_reg_vu0 pragmas have no effect. | N206 (2026-10-27) | **b119p prologue-scheduling and epilogue delay-slot rules** [C] (sdra; probes build/vt/sdra/p1-p11, notes.txt, constsweep.log). (1) A load through a pointer-to-const -- a `const T*` parameter, `this` in a `const` method, `*(T**)` of a `const void*`, a `const` by-value aggregate, a `.lit4` literal -- is never kept behind a store, and the prologue `sd ra` counts as a store; loads through non-const pointers / non-const globals stay after `sd ra` (p11: applies to any store; the new order can renumber registers; `sd ra` may land in a branch delay slot). Same under b103 (not a version effect); load count, leaf-ness, frame size and pragmas explain nothing. (2) Of the branches to the epilogue, only the first in layout order gets `ld ra` in its delay slot; later ones keep `nop`; a branch into an explicit `return;` block is never filled (it stays `b epi; nop` if the block can't be removed) -- so `break` vs `return` and a trailing `return;` decide which exit shows `b; ld ra`. 10 matches queued (StartLeadOut, func_001549F0, func_00301B00, GetChallengeType, Update__17CB3OnlineMenuPage, Update__25CB3OnlineSelNetConfigPage, func_00382FC0, SetTexture__18CB3VehicleRenderer, func_003CD920, func_002F9690 -- the last two from a const-on-every-parameter sweep over all archives), 9 archives improved (func_00467FE0 117 -> 27). | build/vt/sdra/ |

Retail's early `t` register is a call-site shape: `t` as a float local declared just before the statement (N183 kept local; func_0031BD60 needs it before `base = ...`) [C]; doing it in the header (`"+r"(t)`) breaks computed-t sites. Applied; gate 3442 SAME, MATCHES RETAIL. HelpBar func_0031BD60 / func_0031BF40 exact and queued (old DebugMenu drafts removed); reroutes: func_00394280 13 -> 6, func_003350F0 42 -> 13, func_00394660 25 -> 13, func_00394870 43 -> 23, func_003D8920 18 -> 5. Body: trace = R.X()+U.Y()+A.Z(); if > 0: s = Sqrt(trace + 1.0f) (FPU add), h = 0.5f*s first, SetX(U.Z()-A.Y()), SetY(A.X()-R.Z()), SetZ(R.Y()-U.X()), mRot *= 0.5f/s, SetW(h); else a nested-if i (U.Y() > R.X() ? (A.Z() > U.Y() ? 2 : 1) : (A.Z() > R.X() ? 2 : 0)) and switch i: s = Sqrt(rI.I() - (rJ.J() + rK.K()) + 1.0f) (VU add), SetI(0.5f*s), s = 0.5f/s, SetW((rJ.K()-rK.J())*s), SetJ((rI.J()+rJ.I())*s), SetK((rI.K()+rK.I())*s). | N281 (2026-10-31) | **N276 PROVEN, sharper: MWCC's stack-slot layout rule** [C] (gapM; build/vt/gapM/ pr/t1-t7.cpp, notes s.1). (a) every memory object gets its own slot (disjoint scopes don't share); slots fill from the bottom of the local area upward in reverse creation order (first-created highest); the -O4 layout = the -O0 inline layout minus register-promoted objects. (b) creation order by inline LEVEL: all body objects, then all first-level inline objects, then second-level...; -inline bottomup/deferred/auto give the same order. (c) within a level: named locals first (declaration order, block scopes included), then that body's expression temporaries. (d) a class-returning call's result temp is created before its argument temps; by-value temporary arguments are the CALLER's temps ([result, arg x, arg y] per statement at the caller's level); a copy of an lvalue argument is the callee's parameter, first in the callee's level. (e) inlines from one body go statement by statement, calls nested in arguments expanded first -- except when the inline call is the root of an expression statement or a `return` (outer call's locals first). (f) a lane inline's by-value argument temps stay in memory (dead slots) only when its expanded body contains an "=m" asm; otherwise everything is promoted. | build/vt/gapM/ |
| N282 (2026-10-31) | **N255 mechanism explained** [C layout / L cause] (gapM). Our header gives each Y/Z/W product [result, x, y] at the product's level (all dead) plus operator float's copy one level down (the live one) -> live copies pack at the bottom, stride 16. Retail: the same triple, but the live store goes into the RESULT temp and the lane is read straight from it -> [live, dead, dead], stride 48. A mock reproduces retail exactly; sp-direct reads need `const float* p = (const float*)&lVector; float f = p[1]; return f;`. func_002622A0 with that read form + a named-return lane op* (var_F7QOPN.diff): 82/82, 16 changed (live slots stride 48; residue = stack offsets: retail has 25 slots between `at` and the triples vs our 7, and 1 below vs our 9). But it breaks plain lane reads (30 proven DIFF in a scoped headsafe; SetPos__16CB3Globe2dObject 2 -> 10) -> no single header body on b119p [C]. [L] retail's compiler treats the product's result temp differently (cf. N246 NRVO) -> compiler-patch candidate. POD Y/Z/W forms are map-exact at -O0 (Y() 52, Y*Y 80, Y float 36) but promote everything on b119p. func_0044D6D0's slot map: build/vt/gapM/d6_slotmap.txt (0x118-0x218 a permutation of 16-byte objects; levers: rules c, d, e). | build/vt/gapM/notes.txt |

| N293 (2026-11-02) | **w64: 64-bit values passed through `int` declarations (port7's func_00137F20 class)** [C]. **Class:** MWCC/EE keeps integers in 64-bit GPRs and converts only where the declared types differ (int<->long gives dsll32+dsra32, unsigned int->unsigned long dsll32+dsrl32: build/variants/w64_c/w64_d.cpp), so a 64-bit value that goes from a call result (or an argument) into another call's argument (or the return) with **every hop declared 32-bit** compiles to the same bytes as the all-64-bit source; a host compiler keeps 32 bits (or passes garbage upper halves through a mismatched extern "C" prototype). func_00137F20: `static int GetStageGtID(int, int)` + `extern "C" int func_003E9AD0(int)`, but GetStageGtID returns `ld v0` [C retail 0x3EA0D8] and func_003E9AD0 compares a0 with 64-bit `ld` values [C 0x3E9AD8] -- port7-save's 0-vs-1 stage flag. Values in the class: the hard-coded list ids (GtIDs: race vehicles, tracks, race/crash stages; packName40's packed names, func_0021AB40/ADF0 unpack them through __umoddi3/__udivdi3) and the prompt flags (func_0031ECD0 stores a1 with a plain sd and tests bits >= 32 [C 0x31ED08, 0x31EE4C]). **Tools** (build/vt/w64/): retail.py tags every register flow-sensitively (argument / call result / 64-bit producer: ld, d-ops except the dsll32+dsra32 pair, dsrl32 0 zero-extension; sw/ld spills) and closes R64(F) (a 64-bit v0 reaches the return) and P64(F, aN) (an argument reaches a non-stack sd, a 64-bit compare, a d-op or a P64 argument) over the call graph (134 R64 / 160 P64 of 9,173 functions); xcheck.py compares every definition and every callee declaration a source uses against them; scan.py compares declarations with the definition / mangled name; fix.py rewrites widths; cmp.py compiles base vs new outside build/va. **Proven: 123 sources + 3 headers fixed, all byte-identical** (verify_all 3755 SAME, 0 not identical; MATCHES RETAIL; cf06408364): 86 func_0031ECD0 declarations; GtID returns (GetStageGtID, GetCrashStageGtID, GetTrackGtID, GetRaceVehicleGtID, GetCarID, func_0042B700) and arguments (func_003EB060, func_003E9AD0, FindCrashStageName, func_0021AB40/ADF0, func_0013B200/B090/AEF0, func_003A6330, func_0042B010, func_004547C0, func_003EA7F0, IncrementVehicleUsage (mangled FUl; its link-name definition said int)); pass-through definitions that returned `void`/`int` while v0 carries the callee's 64-bit result: GetStageTrackID / GetCrashStageTrackID (now static: the id is p0 in a0, it was read as `this`; return packName40's value), func_003A7060, func_0045FEF0/FF40, func_0013AEB0/B010/B050/A5C0; the locals that carried the values (`int r0`, `int track`, `int id`, ...). | N294 (2026-11-02) | **decompfix: extern "C" prototypes that disagree with their callee (port8's 102 class)** [C]. **Class:** C linkage carries no parameter types and MWCC/EE meets caller and callee only in registers, so a caller may declare `extern "C" void f();` for `f(T *self, int p0)`, pass an `int` where the callee reads a pointer, ignore or invent a return value, and the object still matches retail; a host build that links the two sides breaks (port8: 29 checker failures, the port repo docs/decomp-on-host.md "Calls: port8 changes"). **Checker** `tools/c_linkage_check.py`: every extern "C" declaration in src/b3, src/nonmatching and the headers against the callee's definition (proven first, then archive), else its CodeWarrior-mangled parameter list (+ `this`); argument registers compared per register file (EE: a0-a3/t0-t3 and f12-f19 counted separately, so (P, I, F) = (P, F, I)); an aggregate by value = a pointer to the caller's copy [C proven func_00399E20 reads AddExplosionTrail's CGtV3d args through a1/a2]. Classes kind-IP / kind-IL, fewer, more, ret-callee-void, ret-caller-void, def-* (a definition against its own mangled name). **Authority finding:** the callee's definition is often *not* the authority: tiny_batch lifts type every parameter `int` (BuddyInvite__22CGtBuddiesPS2DirtySockFPcPFbPv_vPv defined (self, int, int, int) against its mangled (char *, fn *, void *); CGtFSM::Construct's `int p0` against `Pv`). Rule used: the mangled name if any, else pointer when either side says pointer and no call passes a non-zero integer literal there, else int (func_00240A60's p0 is the caller's `this`, func_00240E90 stores it [C B3NetworkManager_00271730.cpp:107]). **Fixed, all objects identical** (each touched file compiled before/after outside build/va, tools/obj_same.py; a file whose object changed was restored; gates verify_all 3755 SAME, MATCHES RETAIL; 631c72b9ed, 7e7bd34e5e): 2,293 rows -> 377; port8's list 102 -> 7. Mechanisms (build/vt/clink/): fix_ip.py (pointer/int on both sides; definitions retyped in place or through a local copy `void *p0_` + `int p0 = (int)p0_;`, call arguments cast), fix_dret.py (declared return types = the definition's), fix_ret.py (definitions ending in a tail call return the callee's value: `return self->IsRequestingTalk();` with the callee's own type, bool / unsigned char, else a conversion is emitted), fix_addr.py (declarations only used as `&f` get the full list), fix_pass.py (a call missing registers the callee reads gets the enclosing function's own parameter in that register -- kept only if no move appears, i.e. a pass-through [C]: func_0038D7D0 -> func_00390610's a1/a2, func_00335DB0 -> Free__9CGtMallocFPv's a1), fix_more.py (extra arguments that cost no instruction dropped), fix_ext.py (a definition that never reads registers every caller sets gets them as unread parameters, mostly `this` of a global singleton: OnMemCardPageExit__24CB3MemCardControllerBaseFv, func_00137F20, func_00420B60). **Residue (377 rows, build/vt/clink/final.tsv):** more 245 (the caller sets a register the callee never reads; retail sets it too, so dropping the argument changes bytes -- harmless on a host); fewer 41 (34 calls: the callee reads registers on a path the caller does not set, e.g. func_003FBC20's 15 sound parameters behind `p0 != -1`, func_003FA510; 7 address-only declarations whose full type does not compile against the file's local struct names); kind-IP 35 / 22 names (bodies use the value as an integer, e.g. strncpy/strcasecmp/ustrFormat string pointers declared int; func_0032CF60's p1 gets literals and a pointer); def-more 29 / def-fewer 3 (definitions read registers outside the mangled list: N291 LOCAL class, decomp-modelled pass-through); ret-callee-void 10 (tail calls through a void function pointer or shared-header method: func_00360AE0's ourGlobals.field_134, func_0041E8F0's GetIDOfTalkingNetworkPlayer); kind-IL / def-kind-IL / kind-LP 12 (N293 residue); ret-caller-void 2 (B3PromptsComponent.h header). Not checked: bool vs int returns (same kind I; a host reads only `al` of a bool) -- `--all` lists kind differences beyond void. | tools/c_linkage_check.py, build/vt/clink/ |

| N304 (2026-11-06) | **spimdisasm 1.42.4 (rabbitizer) rejects the R5900 three-operand `multu rd, rs, rt` (rd != 0) as an invalid instruction and then emits the WHOLE function as `.word`s with no size** [C]. `mult rd,rs,rt`, `mult1`, `multu1`, `pextlw` decode fine; MWCC's inline 64-bit multiply (`dsll32/dsra32` lane split, `multu rd`, `mfhi`, `pextlw`, `mult1`, `mult`) is what triggers it. Evidence: func_0021AC70 (`multu $a1,$a3,$a2` 0x21AD28) and func_00376570 (0x37662C / 0x3766C8 / 0x376744) were the only game functions so emitted; func_001060F8 (libmpeg) is the same failure for another reason (a 16-byte constant inside a hand-written routine, decoded as invalid `dsra32` with rs != 0). GNU as `-march=r5900` assembles all of them; objdump `-m mips:5900` decodes them. Fixed by hand (9f827c8ff5, 7cebd59d00, 4363970d8f): instruction text restored, `nonmatching <f>, <size>` + `endlabel`, bytes checked [C] by linking each at its address against orig/SLUS_210.50.rom. Scan 2026-11-06 over assembly/asm: no other sizeless .text function (single `.word`s inside sized functions are deliberate: cvt.w.s, VU ops; the one sizeless .text label left is the dirtysock pointer table D_0041BCF0, data). **A future re-split must keep these three hand listings or re-check for `invalid instruction` runs.** Both game functions then matched (func_0021AC70 proven 40fb11fac3; func_00376570 queued 2026-11-06). | assembly/asm/game/unit_0021AC70.s, unit_00376570.s, assembly/asm/sce/libmpeg.s |
